Appendix 0.C
Proofs of the Results in §
4

0.C.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

First, consider any
g:{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{d}\to{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{n}
such that
for every
\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(\mathbf{x}_{0})
,

\displaystyle g(\mathbf{x})=(\underbrace{0,\ldots,0}_{\mathclap{\text{$\mathrm{class}(f(\mathbf{x}_{0}))-1$}}},1,0,\ldots,0).

(24)

Then,
g
makes the same prediction as
f
on
\mathcal{X}
by
Equation

24
,
and
g
is
\delta
-robust on
\mathcal{X}
since
f
does so.
For each
i\in[n]
, let
g_{i}:{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{d}\to{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}
be the
i
-th component of
g
:
g_{i}(\mathbf{x})\coloneq g(\mathbf{x})_{i}
.
Then, by
Theorem

3.1
, there exist
\sigma
-neural networks
\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n}:{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{d}\to{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}
such that
for every
i\in[n]
and
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbb{I}^{d}
in
[-1,1]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\gamma(\nu_{i}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}))=\big[\min g_{i}(\gamma(\mathcal{B})),\max g_{i}(\gamma(\mathcal{B}))\big]\cap{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}.

(25)

Next, define
\nu:{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{d}\to{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}{}^{n}
by a
\sigma
-neural network that stacks up
\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n}
such that
for every
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbb{I}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})=(\nu_{1}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}),\ldots,\nu_{n}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})).

(26)

We can construct such
\nu
because
\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{n}
have the same depth by the proof of
Theorem

3.1
.
Then,
\nu
makes the same prediction as
g
, and thus as
f
, by
Equations

25
and
26
.
Moreover, we claim that
\nu
is
\delta
-provably robust on
\mathcal{X}
.
To prove this, let
\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{B}\in\mathbb{I}^{d}
with
\gamma(\mathcal{B})=\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(\mathbf{x}_{0})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\gamma(\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}))

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\big[\min g_{i}(\gamma(\mathcal{B})),\max g_{i}(\gamma(\mathcal{B}))\big]\cap{\overline{\mathbb{F}}}

(27)

\displaystyle={\{0\}\times\cdots\times\{0\}}\times\{1\}\times\{0\}\times\cdots\times\{0\},

(28)

where the first equality is by
Equations

25
and
26

and the second equality is by
Equation

24
.
Since
\gamma(\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}))
is a singleton set,

\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\gamma(\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}))
clearly implies
\mathrm{class}(\mathbf{y})=\mathrm{class}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
, as desired.

\blacksquare

0.C.2
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Theorem

4.2
is a direct corollary of
Lemma

3
and the following lemma.

\blacksquare

Lemma 11

Let
\sigma:\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
be the identity function, i.e.,
\sigma(x)=x
for all
x\in\mathbb{F}
.
Then,
\sigma
is
(\mathbb{F},1,1,L_{\phi},L_{\psi})
-separable for some
L_{\phi},L_{\psi}\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

We define
f_{0}
as

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)\coloneq\sigma\left(2^{-1}\otimes\sigma\left(x\oplus\omega\right)\right).

Then we have

\displaystyle f_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle 0,0\rangle)

\displaystyle=\langle 0,0\rangle,\quad f_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle\omega,\omega\rangle)=\langle\omega,\omega\rangle,

\displaystyle f_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle-2\omega,0\rangle)

\displaystyle=\langle 0,0\rangle,\quad f_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle 2\omega,4\omega\rangle)=\langle 2\omega,2\omega\rangle.

Case 1:
x>\omega
with
x=n\cdot\omega
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Define
\mathcal{I}_{i}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneq[2,2^{M+2}-1],

(29)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{k}

\displaystyle\coloneq[2^{M+1+k},2^{M+2+k}-1],\quad k\in\mathbb{N}.

(30)

If
n\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(2^{-1}\otimes\sigma\left((n+2)\omega\right)\right)\leq\left({\tfrac{n}{2}+\tfrac{3}{2}}\right)\omega.

Since the solution of the recurrence relation
a_{i+1}=\tfrac{1}{2}a_{i}+\tfrac{3}{2}
is
a_{i}=(a_{0}-3)(\tfrac{1}{2})^{i}+3
,
pick
m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle m_{1}\geq\left\lceil\frac{\log(2^{M+2}-4)}{\log(2)}\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}}.

we have

\displaystyle a_{m}=(a_{0}-3)(\tfrac{1}{2})^{m_{1}}+3\leq(2^{M+2}-4)(\tfrac{1}{2})^{m_{1}}+3\leq 1+3=4,

which leads to
f^{\circ(m_{1})}(x)\leq 4\omega
and
f^{\circ(m_{1}+2)}(x)=\omega
for
2\omega\leq x<2^{M+2}
.

If
n\in\mathcal{I}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(2^{-1}\otimes\sigma\left(n\omega\right)\right)=\tfrac{n}{2}\omega.

Hence
f_{0}(x)=n_{2}\omega
for
n_{2}\in\mathcal{I}_{k-1}
.

Therefore we have
f_{0}^{\circ(\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-1)}(x)=n_{3}\omega
for
n_{3}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
which leads to

f_{0}^{\circ(\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+m_{1}+1)}(x)=\omega

for
\omega\leq x\leq\Omega
.

Case 2:
x\leq 0
with
x=-n\omega
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

If
n\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(2^{-1}\otimes\sigma\left((-n)\omega\right)\right)\geq(-\tfrac{n}{2}-\tfrac{1}{2})\omega.

Since the solution of the recurrence relation
b_{i+1}=\tfrac{1}{2}b_{i}+\tfrac{1}{2}
is
b_{i}=(a_{0}-1)(\tfrac{1}{2})^{i}+1
,
pick
m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle m_{2}\geq\left\lceil\frac{\log(2^{M+2}-2)}{\log(2)}\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}},

we have

\displaystyle a_{m}=(a_{0}-1)\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)^{m_{1}}+1\leq(2^{M+2}-2)\left({\tfrac{1}{2}}\right)^{m_{1}}+1\leq 2,

which leads to
f^{\circ(m_{2})}(x)\geq-2\omega
and
f^{\circ(m_{1}+1)}(x)=0
for
2^{M+2}<x\leq 0
.

If
n\in\mathcal{I}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(2^{-1}\otimes\sigma\left(-n\omega\right)\right)=-\tfrac{n}{2}\omega.

Therefore we have
f_{0}^{\circ(\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-1)}(x)=-n_{4}\omega
for
n_{4}\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
which leads to

f_{0}^{\circ(\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+m_{2}+1)}(x)=\omega

for
-\Omega\leq x\leq 0
.
We define
g_{0}
as
f_{0}(x)\coloneq f^{\circ o(\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+\max\{m_{1},m_{2}\}+1)}
, and we have

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle-\Omega,0\rangle)

\displaystyle=\langle 0,0\rangle,\quad g_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle\omega,\Omega\rangle)=\langle\omega,\omega\rangle,\quad g_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle)=\langle 0,\omega\rangle.

For
z\in\mathbb{F}
. we define
g_{z}
as

\displaystyle g_{z}(x)\coloneq\begin{cases}g_{0}\left(\sigma(x\ominus z)\right)\quad&\text{if}\quad|z|<2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-M-1},\\
g_{0}\left(\sigma(2^{-1}\otimes x\ominus\frac{z}{2}\right)\quad&\text{if}\quad|z|\geq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-M-1},\end{cases}

and we have

\displaystyle g_{z}^{\sharp}(\langle-\Omega,z\rangle)

\displaystyle=\langle 0,0\rangle,\quad g_{z}^{\sharp}(\langle z^{+},\Omega\rangle)=\langle\omega,\omega\rangle,\quad g_{0}^{\sharp}(\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle)=\langle 0,\omega\rangle.

Finally we define
\iota_{>z}
as

\displaystyle\iota_{>z}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneq\sigma\left(2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\otimes\sigma\left(2^{-M}\otimes g_{z}(x)\right)\right),

\displaystyle\iota_{\geq z}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneq\sigma\left(2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\otimes\sigma\left(2^{-M}\otimes g_{z^{-}}(x)\right)\right),

\displaystyle\iota_{<z}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneq\sigma\left(2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\otimes\sigma\left(2^{-M}\otimes g_{-z}(-x)\right)\right),

\displaystyle\iota_{\leq z}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneq\sigma\left(2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\otimes\sigma\left(2^{-M}\otimes g_{(-z)^{-}}(-x)\right)\right).

∎

Appendix 0.D
Proofs of the Results in §
5.1

In
Lemma

2
, we suppose
\sigma
satisfies
1
. By
Lemma

10
, we suppose
\sigma
satisfies
2
, the relaxed version of
1
.

0.D.1
Proof of Lemma
4

Since
\sigma
also satisfies
2
, it is sufficient to prove the following lemma (
Lemma

12
).
To prove
Lemma

12
, we need following preliminary technical lemmas:
Lemmas

15
,
16
and
17
(presented in
Section

0.F.2
) and
Lemma

14
(presented in
Section

0.F.1
).

\blacksquare

Lemma 12

Suppose that
\sigma:\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
satisfies
2
.
Then, there exists a
\sigma
-network
f
without the first and last affine layer such that

\displaystyle f^{\sharp}\left(\langle-\Omega,\eta\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)\rangle,\quad f^{\sharp}\left(\langle\eta^{+},\Omega\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta^{+}),\sigma(\eta^{+})\rangle.

(31)

Proof.

Let
e_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
2^{e_{0}}\leq|\sigma(\eta^{+})-\sigma(\eta)|<2^{e_{0}+1}
.
Then note that

\displaystyle\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}-M-1\leq e_{0}\leq\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}+1.

Define
\mathfrak{e}_{\theta},\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\tilde{\lambda}
as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}

\displaystyle\coloneq\max\left({\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M,\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-e_{0}-M+1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}

\displaystyle\coloneq\begin{cases}\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-1&\text{ if }\eta>0\;\text{or}\;\eta<0,\eta\neq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}},\\
\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-2&\text{ if }\eta<0,\eta=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}

\displaystyle\coloneq\lambda\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2}.

To use
Lemma

17
, we need to check the assumptions of
Lemma

17
:
\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}\leq-3
and
e_{0}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-3-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}
.
Since
\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}\geq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+5
, we have

\displaystyle-e_{0}+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M+1\leq-\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+2\leq-3.

Therefore we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}\leq-3
.
To show
e_{0}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-3-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}
, first suppose
e_{0}\geq 1
. Then we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}=\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M
, which leads to

\displaystyle e_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}+1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-3=\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-3-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}.

Next suppose
e_{0}\leq 0
. Then we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}=\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-e_{0}-M+1
which leads to

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-3-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-3-(\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-e_{0}-M+1)

\displaystyle\geq(\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+5)-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+e_{0}-4>e_{0}.

We can use
Lemma

17
and consider the following cases.

Case 1:
\sigma(\eta)<\sigma(\eta^{+})
.

By
Lemma

17
, there exists an affine transformation
g
such that

\displaystyle g^{\sharp}\left(\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle\right)\subset\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle,\quad g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{I})=\langle\eta,\eta\rangle,\quad g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{I}^{+})=\langle\eta^{+},\eta^{+}\rangle,

(32)

where

\mathcal{I}\coloneq\langle\sigma(\eta)-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}},\sigma(\eta)\rangle,\mathcal{I}^{+}\coloneq\langle\sigma(\eta^{+}),\sigma(\eta)+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\rangle_{\mathbb{F}}.

In addition, if
x-\sigma(\eta)>2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}
,

g(x)-\eta^{+}\leq\left({x-\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)}\right)\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2},

(33)

and if
x<\sigma(\eta)-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}
,

\eta-g(x)\leq\left({\sigma\left({\eta}\right)-x}\right)\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2},

(34)

Since
e_{0}\geq\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}-M-1
, we have

\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}\leq\max\left({\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M,\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-\max\left\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\right\}+2}\right),

which leads to

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}

\displaystyle=\lambda\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2}\leq\lambda\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+4}\cdot 2^{\max\{-M-2,-\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}\}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-1}\cdot 2^{\min\{\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\},M+2\}}\cdot 2^{\max\{-M-2,-\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta)},\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma(\eta^{+})}\}\}}

\displaystyle\leq 1/2.

Define
g_{1}
as

g_{1}(x)\coloneq\sigma(g(x)).

Then, if
x\geq\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)
, by
Equation

33
,

\displaystyle g_{1}(x)-g_{1}\left({\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\sigma\left(g(x)\right)-\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)\leq\lambda(g(x)-\eta^{+})

\displaystyle\leq\lambda{\left({x-\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)}\right)}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2}\leq\tilde{\lambda}{\left({x-\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)}\right)}.

Similarly, if
x\leq\sigma(\eta)
, by
Equation

34
,

g_{1}\left(\sigma(\eta)\right)-g_{1}(x)\leq\tilde{\lambda}\left(\sigma(\eta)-x\right).

Therefore, we define
n_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\displaystyle n_{1}\coloneq\max\left\{\left\lceil\log_{\tilde{\lambda}^{-1}}\left(\frac{\Omega-\sigma(\eta^{+})}{\sigma(\eta)-\sigma(\eta^{+})+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}}\right)\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}},\left\lceil\log_{\tilde{\lambda}^{-1}}\left(\frac{\sigma(\eta)+\Omega}{2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma}}}\right)\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}}\right\}.

Note that since
\sigma(\eta)-\sigma(\eta^{+})+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\geq-2^{e_{0}}+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}>0
,
n
is well-defined.

We define
h_{1}(x)
as
h_{1}(x)\coloneq g_{1}^{\circ n_{1}}(x)
. Then we have

\displaystyle h_{1}(x)-h_{1}\left(\sigma(\eta^{+})\right)\leq\tilde{\lambda}^{n}\left(x-\sigma(\eta^{+})\right)\leq\sigma(\eta)-\sigma(\eta^{+})+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\;

\displaystyle\text{ if }\;\sigma(\eta^{+})\leq x\leq\Omega,

\displaystyle h_{1}\left(\sigma(\eta)\right)-h_{1}(x)\leq\tilde{\lambda}^{n}\left(\sigma(\eta)-x\right)\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma}}\;

\displaystyle\text{ if }\;-\Omega\leq x\leq\sigma(\eta),

leading to

\displaystyle h_{1}(x)\leq\sigma(\eta)+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{ if }\quad\sigma(\eta^{+})\leq x\leq\Omega,

\displaystyle h_{1}(x)\geq\sigma(\eta)-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma}}\quad

\displaystyle\text{ if }\quad-\Omega\leq x\leq\sigma(\eta).

Finally, we define
f_{1}(x)
as
f_{1}(x)\coloneq g_{1}\circ h_{1}\circ\sigma(x)=g_{1}^{\circ(n+1)}\circ\sigma(x)
. Together with
Equation

32
, we have

\displaystyle f_{1}^{\sharp}\left(\langle-\Omega,\eta\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)\rangle,\quad f_{1}^{\sharp}\left(\langle\eta^{+},\Omega\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta^{+}),\sigma(\eta^{+})\rangle.

Case 2:
\sigma(\eta)>\sigma(\eta^{+})
.

By
Lemma

17
, there exists an affine transformation
g
such that

\displaystyle g^{\sharp}\left(\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle\right)\subset\langle-\Omega,\Omega\rangle,\quad g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{I})=\langle\eta,\eta\rangle,\quad g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{I}^{+})=\langle\eta^{+},\eta^{+}\rangle.

(35)

where

\theta\coloneq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}},\mathcal{I}^{+}\coloneq\langle\sigma(\eta)-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}},\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)\rangle,\quad\mathcal{I}\coloneq\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\rangle,

In addition,

\displaystyle\eta-g(x)\leq\left({x-\sigma\left({\eta}\right)}\right)\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2}\quad\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad\sigma(\eta)+2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\leq x\in\mathbb{F},

(36)

\displaystyle g(x)-\eta^{+}\leq\left({\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)-x}\right)\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}+2}\quad

\displaystyle\text{for}\quad\sigma(\eta)-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\geq x\in\mathbb{F},

(37)

Define
g_{2}
as

g_{2}(x)\coloneq\sigma(g(x)).

Then, if
\sigma\left({\eta}\right)\leq x\in\mathbb{F}
, by
Equation

36
we have

g_{2}\left(\sigma(\eta)\right)-g_{2}(x)\leq\tilde{\lambda}\left(x-\sigma(\eta)\right)\leq\tilde{\lambda}\left(x-\sigma(\eta^{+})\right).

If
\sigma(\eta^{+})\geq x\in\mathbb{F}
, by
Equation

37
we have

\displaystyle g_{2}(x)-g_{2}\left({\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)}\right)\leq\tilde{\lambda}{\left({\sigma\left({\eta^{+}}\right)-x}\right)}\leq\tilde{\lambda}{\left({\sigma\left({\eta}\right)-x}\right)}.

We define
h
as
h\coloneq g_{2}\circ g_{2}(x)
. Then we have

\displaystyle h_{2}^{\sharp}\left(\mathcal{I}_{2}\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta^{+}),\sigma(\eta^{+})\rangle,\quad h_{2}^{\sharp}\left(\mathcal{I}_{2}^{+}\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)\rangle,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}=\langle\sigma(\eta)-\tilde{\lambda}^{-2}\left(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\right),\sigma(\eta^{+})\rangle,\;\mathcal{I}_{2}^{+}=\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)+\tilde{\lambda}^{-2}\left(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}\right)\rangle.

Hence
Therefore, we define
n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
such that

\displaystyle n_{2}\coloneq\max\left\{\left\lceil\log_{\tilde{\lambda}^{-2}}\left(\frac{\Omega-\sigma(\eta^{+})}{2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\theta}}}\right)\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}},\left\lceil\log_{\tilde{\lambda}^{-2}}\left(\frac{\sigma(\eta)+\Omega}{2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\sigma}}}\right)\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}}\right\},

and define
f_{2}
as
f_{2}(x)\coloneq g_{2}\circ h_{2}^{\circ(n_{2})}\circ\sigma(x)=g_{2}^{\circ(2n_{2}+1)}\circ\sigma(x)
.
Together with
Equation

35
, we have

\displaystyle f_{2}^{\sharp}\left(\langle-\Omega,\eta\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta),\sigma(\eta)\rangle,\quad f_{2}^{\sharp}\left(\langle\eta^{+},\Omega\rangle\right)=\langle\sigma(\eta^{+}),\sigma(\eta^{+})\rangle,

and this completes the proof.
∎

0.D.2
Proof of Lemma
5

Since
\sigma
also satisfies
2
, we have
|\sigma(\mathfrak{e}_{\eta})||\in[(1+2^{-M+1}\times 2^{-M-2},1+2^{-2}-2^{-M}]
leading to the fact
\sigma(\eta)
is normal. In addition, we have

\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\theta},\mathfrak{e}_{\theta^{\prime}}\}\geq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+1
since
\max\{|\theta|,|\theta^{\prime}|\}\geq\tfrac{\omega}{\varepsilon}
.

By the lemma below (
Lemma

13
), we have the desired result.
To prove
Lemma

13
, we need following preliminary technical lemmas:
Lemmas

18
,
19
,
20
and
21
(presented in
Section

0.F.3
) and
Lemma

14
(presented in
Section

0.F.1
).

\blacksquare

Lemma 13

Suppose that
\sigma:\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
satisfies
2
.
Let
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
with
|\kappa_{1}|<|\kappa_{2}|
with
\mathfrak{e}_{\kappa_{2}}\geq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+1
. Suppose there exist
c_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
K\coloneq\sigma(c_{2})
is normal.
Then, there exist
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
w_{1},\alpha_{i},z_{i},b\in\mathbb{F}
such that

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.02228pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b

\displaystyle=\kappa_{1},

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.02228pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b

\displaystyle=\kappa_{2}.

Proof.

By
Lemma

14
, either
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\in(2^{-1},1]_{\mathbb{F}}
or
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\in[2^{-1},1)_{\mathbb{F}}
exists such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{K}

\displaystyle=1^{-}=1-2^{-M-1}=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\text{ times }},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{K}

\displaystyle=1.

Let define
\tilde{\kappa}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}\oplus\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}.

Since
\mathfrak{e}_{\kappa_{2}}\geq 1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}
, we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}\geq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}
.
Let
\sigma(\bar{\gamma})=\max\{|\sigma(\gamma_{1})|,|\sigma(\gamma_{2})|\}
.

By
Lemma

18
, there exists
w_{1}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

\displaystyle w_{1}\otimes\left(\sigma(\gamma_{2})\ominus\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+\epsilon_{1}=\tilde{\kappa},\quad\mathfrak{e}_{w_{1}}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}-e_{0},

for some
\epsilon_{1}=0
or
\pm 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}}
.
Then by
Lemma

19
,

\displaystyle w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})=w_{1}\otimes\left(\sigma(\gamma_{2})\ominus\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+(C\times 2^{-M-1}),

where

\displaystyle|C|

\displaystyle\leq|w_{1}|\left((2+2^{-M-1})|\sigma(\eta^{+})+\sigma(\eta)|+|\sigma(\gamma_{2})|+|\sigma(\gamma_{1})|\right)

\displaystyle\leq(6+2^{-M})|w_{1}||\sigma(\bar{\gamma})|\leq 7\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}+1}}.

Hence we have

\displaystyle w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})=w_{1}\otimes\left(\sigma(\gamma_{2})\ominus\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+\epsilon_{2},

for some
|\epsilon_{2}|\leq 7\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}}
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})=\tilde{\kappa}+\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)+\epsilon_{3},

where
|\epsilon_{3}|\leq 8\times 2^{-M+\tilde{\kappa}}
. Since
M\geq 3
, the exponents of
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})
and
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})
are at most
\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}+2
.

We consider the following cases.

Case 1:
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}
exists.

Let
\zeta=w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})
. For
i=0,\dots,M
, we define
\alpha_{i},z_{i},b
as

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i},z_{i})=\begin{cases}(0,c_{2})\;&\text{if}\;\mathfrak{s}_{\zeta,1}=0\\
(-\text{sign}(\zeta)\times\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\times 2^{-i+\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},c_{2})\;&\text{if}\;\mathfrak{s}_{\zeta,1}=1\end{cases},\quad b=\kappa_{1}.

Then since
\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)=\zeta
, we have

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=0.

Since the exponents of
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})
and
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})
are at most
\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}+2
, we have

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)=\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)+\zeta+\epsilon_{4},\quad|\epsilon_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}+2},

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b=\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{5},\quad|\epsilon_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq 12\times 2^{-M+\tilde{\kappa}}.

By
Lemma

20
, there exist
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{36}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{36}\delta_{i}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{4}\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{36}\delta_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\kappa},

where
\delta_{i}=\pm 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\delta_{i}}}
.
Hence, let
(\alpha_{i+M},z_{i+M})=(\text{sign}(\delta_{i})\times\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\delta_{i}}},c_{2})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=M+1}^{M+37}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b=\kappa_{1},

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{M}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=M+1}^{M+37}\oplus b=(\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{2})\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=M+1}^{M+37}\delta_{i}\oplus\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\kappa}\oplus\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}.

Case 2:
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}
exists.

Let
\zeta_{0}=w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})
.
We recursively define
\zeta_{i},\alpha_{i},z_{i}
as

\displaystyle(\alpha_{i},z_{i})

\displaystyle\coloneq(\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\dagger}\times 2^{-i+\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta_{i-1}}},c_{2}),\quad\zeta_{i}\coloneq\zeta_{i-1}\ominus 0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\text{ times }}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta_{i-1}}},\;b=\kappa_{1}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\text{ times }}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta_{i-1}}}.

Let
n_{2}=\min\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta_{i}}\leq-4-2M+\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}\}
.

Note that
\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)=\zeta\pm 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-M}
. Then we have

\displaystyle|\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)|\leq(2-2^{-M})\times 2^{-4-2M+\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}}.

Since the exponents of
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})
and
w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})
are at most
\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}+2
, we have

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)=\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)+\zeta+\epsilon_{6},\quad

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{6}|\leq 2\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\tilde{\kappa}}+2},

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b=\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{7},\quad

\displaystyle|\epsilon_{7}|\leq 16\times 2^{-M+\tilde{\kappa}}.

By
Lemma

21
, there exist
\delta_{1},\dots,\delta_{48}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

\displaystyle|\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)|\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{48}\delta_{i}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{7}\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{48}\delta_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\kappa},

where
\delta_{i}=\pm 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\delta_{i}}}
.
Hence let
(\alpha_{i+M},z_{i+M})=(\text{sign}(\delta_{i})\times\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\dagger}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\delta_{i}}},c_{2})
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{1})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=n_{2}+1}^{n_{2}+49}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus b=\kappa_{1},

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}\otimes\sigma(\gamma_{2})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n_{2}}\left(\alpha_{i}\otimes\sigma(z_{i})\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=n_{2}+1}^{n_{2}+49}\oplus b=(\tilde{\kappa}+\epsilon_{2})\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=n_{2}+1}^{n_{2}+49}\delta_{i}\oplus\kappa_{1}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\kappa}\oplus\kappa_{1}=\kappa_{2}.

∎

0.D.3
Proof of Lemma
6

To prove
Lemma

6
, we need the following preliminary technical lemma:
Lemma

23
(presented in
Section

0.F.4
) and
Lemma

14
(presented in
Section

0.F.1
)

Define
\mu_{z}(\cdot)
as
\mu_{z}(x)=(w\otimes x)\oplus b
. Since
\eta\in[-4+8\varepsilon,4-8\varepsilon]_{\mathbb{F}},|\eta|\geq\tfrac{\omega}{\varepsilon}
we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\eta^{-}},\mathfrak{e}_{\eta},\mathfrak{e}_{\eta^{+}}\leq 1
.

We represent
\eta,z
as
\eta=\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
,
z=\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
with
1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}\leq e_{\eta}\leq 1
.

Let
c_{z}=\max\{\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-\mathfrak{e}_{z},0\}\geq 0
. Note that
\mathfrak{e}_{z}+c_{z}\geq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}.

By
Lemma

14
, at least one of
\mathfrak{s}_{z}^{\parallel}
or
\mathfrak{s}_{z}^{{\ddagger}}
exists.
We consider the following cases.

Case 1:
\eta>0\;z\geq 0
.

In this case, (1) in
Lemma

6
holds if

\displaystyle w>0,\quad\begin{cases}\mu_{z}(-1)&\geq-\Omega,\\
\mu_{z}(z)&=\eta,\\
\mu_{z}(z^{+})&=\eta^{+},\\
\mu_{z}(1)&\leq\Omega.\end{cases}

Note that
z^{+}=(\mathfrak{s}_{z}+2^{-M+c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.
Let

\displaystyle w=2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{z}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}},\quad b=(\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}.

Since
1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}\leq 1
, we have
\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}\leq 1-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}=\mathfrak{e}_{\max}
which leads to
w,b\in\mathbb{F}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{x}}\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+(\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=(\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}})\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({\eta+2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta^{+}.

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)=-w\oplus b

\displaystyle\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)=w\oplus b

\displaystyle\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}\leq\Omega.

Case 2:
\eta<0,\;z<0
.

In this case, (1) in
Lemma

6
holds if

\displaystyle w>0,\quad\begin{cases}\mu_{z}(-1)&\geq-\Omega,\\
\mu_{z}(z)&=\eta,\\
\mu_{z}(z^{+})&=\eta^{+},\\
\mu_{z}(1)&\leq\Omega.\end{cases}

Note that we have
z=-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
and
\eta=-\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
.

Case 2-1:
\eta\neq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
.

In this case, we have
\eta^{+}=-(\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}-2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
.

Case 2-1-1:
-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\leq z<0
, or
z<-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},\;z\neq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.

In this case, we have
z^{+}=-(\mathfrak{s}_{z}-2^{-M+c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.
Let

\displaystyle w=2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{z}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}},\quad b=(-\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}+\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+(-\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}+\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b

\displaystyle=(-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}})\oplus b=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\eta+2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega.

Case 2-1-2:
z<-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},\;z=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.

In this case, we have
1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{z}\leq 1
,
c_{z}=0
and
z^{+}=-(2-2^{-M})\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.
By
Lemma

23
, we have
(1+2^{-M})\otimes z=-(1+2^{-M})\times 2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
and
(1+2^{-M})\otimes(z^{+})=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
. Let

\displaystyle w=(1+2^{-M})\times 2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{z}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}},\quad b=(-\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}+\mathfrak{s}_{z}+2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=-(1+2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({-(1+2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}+(-\mathfrak{s}_{\eta}+1+2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}\oplus b=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\eta+2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega.

Case 2-2:
\eta=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
.

In this case, we have
\eta^{+}=-(2-2^{-M})\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}
.

Case 2-2-1:
-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\leq z<0
, or
z<-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},\;z\neq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.

In this case, we have
z^{+}=-(\mathfrak{s}_{z}-2^{-M+c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
and
(-1+\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1-c_{z}})
is exact.
Let

\displaystyle w=2^{-1-\mathfrak{e}_{z}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}},\quad b=(-1+\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+(-1+\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=(-\mathfrak{s}_{z}\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}})\oplus b

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\eta+2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega.

Case 2-2-2:
z<-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},\;z=-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.

In this case, we have
c_{z}=0
and
z^{+}=-(2-2^{-M})\times 2^{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{z}}
.
Let

\displaystyle w=2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{z}+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}},\quad b=(-1+2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=-2^{-\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}\oplus b=\mathrm{rnd}{({-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+(-1+2^{-c_{z}})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=(-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-c_{z}}+2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}})\oplus b=\mathrm{rnd}{({-\eta+2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}}})}=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega.

Case 3:
\eta>0,\;z<0
.

In this case, (2) in
Lemma

6
holds if

\displaystyle w>0,\quad\begin{cases}\mu_{z}(-1)&\leq\Omega,\\
\mu_{z}(z)&=\eta^{+},\\
\mu_{z}(z^{+})&=\eta,\\
\mu_{z}(1)&\geq-\Omega.\end{cases}

In this case, let
\nu:=-(\eta^{+})
. Using
Case 2
, there exist
w_{\nu},b_{\nu}\in\mathbb{F}
with
w_{\nu}>0
such that

\displaystyle(w_{\nu}\otimes z)\oplus b_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\nu=-(\eta^{+}),

\displaystyle(w_{\nu}\otimes z^{+})\oplus b_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\nu^{+}=-\eta.

Let
w=-w_{\nu},b=-b_{\nu}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b=w_{\nu}\oplus(-b_{\nu})\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b=(-w_{\nu})\oplus(-b_{\nu})\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega.

Case 4:
\eta<0,\;z\geq 0
.

Let
\nu:=-(\eta^{+})
.
Using
Case 1
, there exist
w_{\nu},b_{\nu}
with
w_{n}u>0
such that

\displaystyle(w_{\nu}\otimes z)\oplus b_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\nu=-(\eta^{+}),

\displaystyle(w_{\nu}\otimes z^{+})\oplus b_{\nu}

\displaystyle=\nu^{+}=-\eta.

Let
w=-w_{\nu},b=-b_{\nu}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z)

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z)\oplus b=\eta^{+},

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(z^{+})

\displaystyle=(w\otimes z^{+})\oplus b=\eta,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(-1)

\displaystyle=-w\oplus b=w_{\nu}\oplus(-b_{\nu})\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}<\Omega,

\displaystyle\mu_{z}(1)

\displaystyle=w\oplus b=(-w_{\nu})\oplus(-b_{\nu})\geq-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\max}}>-\Omega.

\blacksquare

Appendix 0.E
Proofs of the Results in §
5.2

Throughout this section, we use
\mathcal{R}=\{x\in\mathbb{F}:|x|\in[\tfrac{\varepsilon}{2}+2\varepsilon^{2},\tfrac{5}{4}-2\varepsilon]_{\mathbb{F}}\}
and
K=\sigma(c_{2})
, i.e.,
K\in\mathcal{R}
by
1
.

We present preliminary technical lemmas for the proofs in this section:
Lemmas

26
,
24
and
25
(presented in
Section

0.F.5
).

0.E.1
Proof of Lemma
7

We prove
Lemma

7
using the mathematical induction on
\lceil\log_{2^{M}}d\rceil
.
Consider the base case where
\lceil\log_{2^{M}}d\rceil=1
, i.e.,
d\in[2^{M}]
. Let
\mathcal{B}=(\langle{a_{1},b_{1}}\rangle,\dots,\langle{a_{d},b_{d}}\rangle
and
\mu_{K,\eta,n}:\mathbb{F}^{n}\to\mathbb{F}
be a network without the first affine layer such that for
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in\{0,K\}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{K,\eta,n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle>\eta\quad\text{if}~x_{i}=K~\text{for all}~i\in[d],

\displaystyle\mu_{K,\eta,n}(x_{1},\dots,x_{d})

\displaystyle\leq\eta\quad\text{otherwise}.

We note that such
\mu_{K,\eta,n}
always exists for all
n\in[2^{M}]
by
Lemma

26
.
We then construct
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbf{x})=\psi_{>\eta}\Big(\mu_{K,\eta,d}\big(\tilde{\nu}_{1}(x_{1}),\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{d}(x_{d})\big)\Big),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{i}(x_{i})=\psi_{>\eta}\left(\mu_{K,\eta,2}\Big(\phi_{\geq a_{i}}(x_{i}),\phi_{\leq b_{i}}(x_{i})\Big)\right)\quad\text{for all}~i\in[d].

Then, one can observe that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}
has depth
L_{\phi}+2L_{\psi}-2
and does not have the last affine layer.

Let
\mathcal{C}=(\langle{s_{1},t_{1}}\rangle,\dots,\langle{s_{d},t_{d}}\rangle)
be an abstract box in
[a,b]_{\mathbb{F}}^{d}
, and let
\bar{\mathcal{B}}=\gamma\left(\mathcal{B}\right)
and
\bar{\mathcal{C}}=\gamma\left(\mathcal{C}\right)
.
We now show that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})=(K\iota_{\mathcal{B}})^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})
by considering the following three cases: (1)
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}=\emptyset
, (2)
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}\neq\emptyset
and
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\not\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
, and (3)
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
.

Case 1:
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}=\emptyset
.

In this case, there exists
i^{*}\in[d]
such that
[s_{i^{*}},t_{i^{*}}]_{\mathbb{F}}\cap[a_{i^{*}},b_{i^{*}}]_{\mathbb{F}}=\emptyset
; otherwise,
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}\neq\emptyset
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{i^{*}}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{i^{*}},t_{i^{*}}}\rangle)=\langle{0,0}\rangle.

Since
\gamma(g_{i}^{\sharp}(\langle{c_{i},d_{i}}\rangle)\subset[0,K]
by the definition of
\psi_{>\eta}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{K,\eta,2}^{\sharp}\Big(\tilde{\nu}_{1}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{1},t_{1}}\rangle),\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{d}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{d},t_{d}}\rangle)\Big)=\langle{u,v}\rangle,

for some
u,v\in\mathbb{F}
such that
u\leq v<\eta
.
Hence,

\tilde{\nu}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})=\psi_{>\eta}\big(\langle{u,v}\rangle\big)=\langle{0,0}\rangle.

Case 2:
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}\neq\emptyset
and
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\not\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
.

Since
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}\neq\emptyset
,
[a_{i},b_{i}]_{\mathbb{F}}\cap[s_{i},t_{i}]_{\mathbb{F}}\neq\emptyset
for all
i\in[d]
. This implies that for each
i\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{i}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{i},t_{i}}\rangle)=\langle{w_{i},1}\rangle,

(38)

for some
w_{i}\in\{0,1\}
.
Furthermore, since
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\not\subset\bar{\mathcal{C}}
, there exists
i^{*}\in[n]
such that
[s_{i^{*}},t_{i^{*}}]_{\mathbb{F}}\not\subset[a_{i^{*}},b_{i^{*}}]_{\mathbb{F}}
, i.e.,
w_{i^{*}}=0
. Combining
Equation

38
and
u_{i^{*}}=0
implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{K,\eta,2}^{\sharp}\Big(\tilde{\nu}_{1}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{1},t_{1}}\rangle,\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{d}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{d},t_{d}}\rangle)\Big)=\langle{u,v}\rangle,

for some
u<\eta<v
. Hence,

\tilde{\nu}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})=\psi_{>\eta}\big(\langle{u,v}\rangle\big)=\langle{0,K}\rangle
.

Case 3:
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
.

Since
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
,
[s_{i},t_{i}]_{\mathbb{F}}\subset[a_{i},b_{i}]_{\mathbb{F}}
for all
i\in[n]
. This implies that

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{i}^{\sharp}(\langle{s_{i},t_{i}}\rangle)=\langle{1,1}\rangle,

for all
i\in[n]
.
Thus, it holds that
\tilde{\nu}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{K,K}\rangle
.
By considering all three cases, one can conclude that when
\lceil\log_{2^{M}}d\rceil=1
, then a depth-
(L_{\phi}+2L_{\psi}-2)

\sigma
-network
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\sharp}=(K\iota_{\mathcal{B}})^{\sharp}
on
[a,b]_{\mathbb{F}}^{d}
.

Now, suppose that
\lceil\log_{2^{M}}d\rceil=n>1
.
Let
k\in[2^{M}-1]
and
r\in[2^{(n-1)M}-1]\cup\{0\}
such that
d=k2^{(n-1)M}+r
, and let

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{j}=\big(\langle{a_{(j-1)2^{(n-1)M}+1},b_{(j-1)2^{(n-1)M}+1}}\rangle,\dots,\langle{a_{j2^{(n-1)M}},b_{j2^{(n-1)M}}}\rangle\big)\quad\text{for all}~j\in[k],

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{k+1}=\big(\langle{a_{k2^{(n-1)M}+1},b_{k2^{(n-1)M}+1}}\rangle,\dots,\langle{a_{k2^{(n-1)M}+r},b_{k2^{(n-1)M}+r}}\rangle\big).

Then, by the inductive hypothesis, there exist depth-
(L_{\phi}+nL_{\phi}-n)

\sigma
-networks
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}},\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k}}
and a depth-
(L_{\phi}+(\lceil\log_{2^{M}}r\rceil+1)L_{\phi}-(\lceil\log_{2^{M}}r\rceil+1))

\sigma
-network
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}
such that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{j}}^{\sharp}=(K\iota_{\mathcal{B}_{j}})^{\sharp}
on
[a,b]_{\mathbb{F}}^{d}
for all
j\in[k+1]
.
Let
\hat{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}
be the composition of
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}
and
(n-1-\lceil\log_{2^{M}}r\rceil)
times composition of
\psi_{>\eta}\circ\mu_{K,\eta,1}
, i.e.,

\hat{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}
is a depth-
(L_{\phi}+nL_{\phi}-n)

\sigma
-network satisfying

\hat{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}^{\sharp}=(K\iota_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}})^{\sharp}
.
Here, we note that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}},\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k}},\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}},\dots,\hat{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}
have the same depth and they do not have the last affine layers.
Under this observation, we construct
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\sharp}=\psi_{>\eta}^{\sharp}\Big(\mu_{K,\eta,k+1}^{\sharp}\big(\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}^{\sharp},\dots,\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k}}^{\sharp},\hat{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}_{k+1}}^{\sharp}\big)\Big).

Then,
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\sharp}=(K\iota_{\mathcal{B}})^{\sharp}
and
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}
has depth
L_{\phi}+(n+1)L_{\psi}-n-1
. This proves the inductive step and completes the proof.

\blacksquare

0.E.2
Proof of Lemma
8

Let
\mathcal{T}
be the collection of all abstract boxes in
\mathcal{S}
. We construct
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})
as

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})=\psi_{>\eta}\big(g(\mathbf{x})\big),\quad g(\mathbf{x})=\left(\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{T}}w\otimes\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbf{x})\right)\oplus\eta,

where
w\in\mathbb{F}
is chosen so that
w\otimes K\in(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M-1},(1+2^{-1})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}-M})_{\mathbb{F}}
, i.e.,
(w\otimes K)\oplus\eta\geq\eta^{+}
. Such
w
always exists since
K\in\mathcal{R}
and by
Lemma

24
.
Furthermore, from our choice of
w
, we note that
\eta\leq g(\mathbf{x})\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\eta}+1}
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{F}
, i.e., overflow does not occur in the evaluation of
g
under
2^{E-1}\geq M\geq 3
. Here, note that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathbf{x})
has depth
L+L_{\phi}-1
.

We now show that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sharp}=(K\iota_{\mathcal{S}})^{\sharp}
on
[a,b]^{d}_{\mathbb{F}}
.
Let
\mathcal{C}=(\langle{s_{1},t_{1}}\rangle,\dots,\langle{s_{d},t_{d}}\rangle)
be an abstract box in
[a,b]_{\mathbb{F}}^{d}
, and let
\bar{\mathcal{C}}=\gamma\left(\mathcal{C}\right)
and
\bar{\mathcal{B}}=\gamma\left(\mathcal{B}\right)
for all
\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{T}
.
Here, if
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\cap\mathcal{S}=\emptyset
, then
\bar{\mathcal{B}}\cap\bar{\mathcal{C}}=\emptyset
for all
\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{T}
. This implies that

\displaystyle g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{\eta,\eta}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})=\langle{0,0}\rangle,

by the definition of
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}
(see
Lemma

7
).
In addition, if
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\cap\mathcal{S}\neq\emptyset
and
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\not\subset\mathcal{S}
, then
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\not\subset\bar{\mathcal{B}}
for all
\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{T}
and there exists
\mathcal{B}^{*}\in\mathcal{T}
such that
\bar{\mathcal{B}}^{*}\cap\mathcal{C}\neq\emptyset
.
This implies that
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{0,u_{\mathcal{B}}}\rangle
for some
u_{\mathcal{B}}\in\{0,K\}
for all
\mathcal{B}\in\mathcal{T}
and
u_{\mathcal{B}^{*}}=K
. This implies that for some
v\geq\eta^{+}
, we have

\displaystyle g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{\eta,v}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\nu}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{B}}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{0,K}\rangle.

Lastly, suppose that
\bar{\mathcal{C}}\subset\mathcal{S}
. Since
\mathcal{C}
is a box in
\mathcal{S}
, this implies
\mathcal{C}\in\mathcal{T}
and
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{K,K}\rangle
. Thus, for some
\eta^{+}\leq u\leq v
, we have

\displaystyle g^{\sharp}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{u,v}\rangle\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\nu}^{\sharp}_{\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{C})=\langle{K,K}\rangle.

This completes the proof.

\blacksquare

0.E.3
Proof of Lemma
9

Let
\mathcal{D}=[a,b]^{d}_{\mathbb{F}}
and
define

\displaystyle h_{+}(\mathbf{x})=\max\{0,h(\mathbf{x})\}\quad\text{and}\quad h_{-}(\mathbf{x})\max\{0,-h(\mathbf{x})\},

i.e.,
h^{\sharp}=h_{+}^{\sharp}\ominus^{\sharp}h_{-}^{\sharp}
.
Let
k_{+},k_{-}\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
be the numbers such that
\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}h_{+}(\mathbf{x})
is the
(k_{+}+1)
-th smallest non-negative number in
\mathbb{F}
and
\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}h_{-}(\mathbf{x})
is the
(k_{-}+1)
-th smallest non-negative number in
\mathbb{F}
.
Let
0=z_{0}<z_{1}<\cdots<z_{(|\mathbb{F}|-1)/2}=\Omega<z_{(|\mathbb{F}|+1)/2}=\infty
be all non-negative numbers in
\mathbb{F}\cup\{\infty\}
; here, we have
z_{k_{+}}=\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}h_{+}(\mathbf{x})
and
z_{k_{-}}=\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}}h_{-}(\mathbf{x})
.

Let
\mathcal{S}_{\tau,i}=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{D}:h_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})\geq z_{i}\}
for
\tau\in\{-,+\}

for all
i\in[(|\mathbb{F}|+1)/2]\cup\{0\}

and define
f:\mathcal{D}\to\mathbb{F}
as

\displaystyle\nu(\mathbf{x})=\left(\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k_{+}}\big(w_{i}\otimes\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{+,i}}(\mathbf{x})\big)\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k_{-}}\big((-w_{i})\otimes\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{-,i}}(\mathbf{x})\big),

where
w_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
is chosen so that
w_{i}\otimes K\in(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z_{i}}-M-1},(1+2^{-1})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z_{i}}-M})_{\mathbb{F}}
. Such
w_{i}
exists for all
i
by
K\in\mathcal{R}
and
Lemma

24
.
Here, we note that
\nu
has depth
L
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a box in
\mathcal{D}
and choose
i_{\tau,\max},i_{\tau,\min}
such that
z_{i_{\tau,\max}}=\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\gamma\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}h_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})
and
z_{i_{\tau,\min}}=\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\gamma\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}h_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})
for
\tau\in\{-,+\}
.
Then, one can observe that for
\tau\in\{-,+\}
and
i\in[(|\mathbb{F}|+1)/2]\cup\{0\}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{\tau,i}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})=\begin{cases}\langle{K,K}\rangle~&\text{if}~i\leq i_{\tau,\min}\\
\langle{0,K}\rangle~&\text{if}~i_{\min}<i\leq i_{\tau,\max}\\
\langle{0,0}\rangle~&\text{if}~i_{\tau,\max}<i\end{cases}.

By the definition of
w_{i}
and
Lemma

25
, this implies that

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{\sharp}}{\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits}_{i=1}^{k_{+}}\big(w_{i}\otimes^{\sharp}\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{+,i}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})\big)=\langle{z_{i_{+,\min}},z_{i_{+,\max}}}\rangle.

Here, if
z_{i_{+,\min}}>0
, then by the definition
h_{-}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\tau,i}
, we have
\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{-,i}}(\mathcal{B})=\langle{0,0}\rangle
for all
i
. This implies that

\displaystyle\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})=\sideset{}{{}^{\sharp}}{\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits}_{i=1}^{k_{+}}\big(w_{i}\otimes^{\sharp}\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{+,i}}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})\big)=\langle{z_{i_{+,\min}},z_{i_{+,\max}}}\rangle=h^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B}).

If
z_{i_{+,\min}}=0
, then

\displaystyle(-w_{i})\otimes^{\sharp}\tilde{\nu}_{\mathcal{S}_{-,i}^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})}=\langle{-u_{i},0}\rangle,

for some
u_{i}\in(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z_{i}}-M-1},(1+2^{-1})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{z_{i}}-M})_{\mathbb{F}}
.
Hence, by
Lemma

25
and the definition of
\oplus^{\sharp}
, we have
\nu^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})=\langle{-z_{i_{-,\max}},z_{i_{+,\max}}}\rangle=h^{\sharp}(\mathcal{B})
.

\blacksquare

Appendix 0.F
Technical Lemmas

0.F.1
Common Technical Lemma

We present the technical lemma used in multiple proofs.

Lemma 14

Suppose
M\geq 3,E\geq 2
. For any
x\in[1,2)_{\mathbb{F}}
, at least one of the followings holds:

\bullet

There exists
y\in(2^{-1},1]_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
x\otimes y=1
. In this case, we denote
y
as
x^{\parallel}\coloneq y
.

\bullet

There exists
y\in[2^{-1},1)_{\mathbb{F}}
such that
x\otimes y_{1}=1^{-}=1-2^{-1-M}
. In this case, we denote
y
as
x^{{\dagger}}\coloneq y_{1}
.

Proof.

If
x=1
, then
x^{\parallel}=1
,
x^{\dagger}=1-2^{-1-M}
.

Now suppose
1<x<2
. Note that
x=1.\underbrace{m_{x,1}...m_{x,M}}_{M\;\text{times}}\times 2^{0}
.
Let
n_{x}=x\times 2^{M}\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that
2^{M}<n_{x}<2^{M+1}
. By dividing
2^{2M}
by
n_{x}
, we have the following

2^{2M}=n_{x}n_{u}+r\quad(0<r<n_{x}).

(39)

Note that since
2^{M-1}\leq n_{u}<2^{M}
,
n_{u}\times 2^{1-M}
has the form of
1.\underbrace{m_{1}...m_{M}}_{M\;\text{times}}\times 2^{-1}
whose significand has
\leq M+1
binary digits.
We consider the following cases.

\bullet

Case (1)

0<r\leq 2^{-2+M}
.

In this case,
x^{\parallel}={n_{u}}\times 2^{-M}
exists since

\displaystyle 1-2^{-2-M}

\displaystyle\leq x\times(n_{u}\times 2^{-M})=(n_{x}\times n_{u})\times 2^{-2M}=1-r\times 2^{-2M}<1.

\bullet

Case (2)

2^{-2+M}<r<n_{x}-2^{-1+M}
.

In this case, note that
n_{x}-2^{-1+M}<2^{-1+M}<3\times 2^{-2+M}
.
Then
x^{\dagger}={n_{u}}\times 2^{-M}
exists since

\displaystyle 1-3\times 2^{-2-M}

\displaystyle<x\times(n_{u}\times 2^{-M})=(n_{x}\times n_{u})\times 2^{-2M}=1-r\times 2^{-2M}<1-2^{-2-M}.

\bullet

Case (3)

n_{x}-2^{-1+M}\leq r<n_{x}
.

In this case,
x^{\parallel}=(n_{u}+1)\times 2^{-M}
exists since

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle<x\times((n_{u}+1)\times 2^{-M})=1+(n_{x}-r)\times 2^{-2M}\leq 1+2^{-1-M}.

Now, we show
x^{\parallel}>2^{-1}
. If
n_{x}\leq 2^{1+M}-4
, we have
n_{u}\geq 2^{-1+M}+1
. If
n_{x}\geq 2^{1+M}-3
, we have
n_{u}=2^{-1+M},r\geq 2^{-1+M}
which does not belong to
Case (1)
.
Therefore we have
x^{\parallel}>2^{-1}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

0.F.2
Technical Lemmas for Lemma
4

This subsection presents technical lemmas for the proof of
Lemma

4
(
Section

0.D.1
).

Lemma 15

Suppose
\eta\in(-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}})_{\mathbb{F}}
. For any normal
x\in(-1-2^{-1},1+2^{-1})_{\mathbb{F}}
, there exist
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
such that
(y_{1}\otimes x)\oplus(y_{2}\otimes x)=\eta
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume
\eta,x>0
.
Since
\eta\in(-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}})_{\mathbb{F}}
, we write
\eta
as
\eta=n_{\eta}\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}
for
1\leq n_{\eta}<2^{1+M},n_{\eta}\in\mathbb{N}
. First define
y_{*}
as

\displaystyle y_{*}\coloneq\begin{cases}2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-\mathfrak{e}_{x}}\quad&\text{if}\quad x<1,\mathfrak{s}_{x}=1,\\
2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-\mathfrak{e}_{x}}\quad&\text{if}\quad x<1,\mathfrak{s}_{x}>1,\\
2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1\leq x<1+2^{-1}.\end{cases}

If
n_{\eta}=1
, let
y_{1}=y_{*}
.

Then we have
y_{1}\otimes x=\begin{cases}\mathrm{rnd}{({2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\quad&\text{if}\quad x<1,\mathfrak{s}_{x}=1,\\
\mathrm{rnd}{({\mathfrak{s}_{x}\times 2^{-1-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\quad&\text{if}\quad x<1,\mathfrak{s}_{x}>1,\\
\mathrm{rnd}{({\mathfrak{s}_{x}\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\quad&\text{if}\quad 1\leq x<1+2^{-1}.\\
\end{cases}

If
n_{\eta}>1
, we have
2\leq n_{\eta}\leq 2^{1+M}-1
.
Since
x
is normal, we can write
x
as
x=n_{x}\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{x}}
for some
2^{M}\leq n_{x}<2^{1+M},n_{x}\in\mathbb{N}
.

By dividing
2^{M}\times n_{\eta}
by
n_{x}
, we have

\displaystyle 2^{M}\times n_{\eta}=m\times n_{x}+r\quad(0\leq r<n_{x}),

for some
1\leq m\leq 2^{1+M}-1
.

Let

\displaystyle(n_{y_{1}},y_{2})=\begin{cases}(m,0)\quad&\text{if}\quad r<2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r=2^{M-1},n_{\eta}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)\\
(m,y_{*})\quad&\text{if}\quad 2^{M-1}<r<3\times 2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r\in\{2^{M-1},3\times 2^{M-1}\},n_{\eta}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)\\
(m+1,0)\quad&\text{if}\quad r>3\times 2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r=3\times 2^{M-1},n_{\eta}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)\end{cases}

and

y_{1}=n_{y_{1}}\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-\mathfrak{e}_{x}}\in\mathbb{F}
.
We consider the following cases.

Case 1:
r<2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r=2^{M-1},n_{\eta}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle y_{1}\otimes x=\mathrm{rnd}{({(n_{y_{1}}\times n_{x}\times 2^{-M})\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=\mathrm{rnd}{({(n_{\eta}-r\times 2^{-M})\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=\eta.

Case 2:
2^{M-1}<r<3\times 2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r\in\{2^{M-1},3\times 2^{M-1}\},n_{\eta}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)
.

In this case, we have

\displaystyle(y_{1}\otimes x)\oplus(y_{2}\otimes x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({(n_{\eta}-r\times 2^{-M})\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}\oplus 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}

\displaystyle=(n_{\eta}-1)\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}\oplus 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}=\eta.

Case 3:
r>3\times 2^{M-1}\;\text{or}\;r=3\times 2^{M-1},n_{\eta}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 2)
.

In this case, since
3\times 2^{M-1}\leq r,n_{x}<2^{M+1}
, we have
0\leq n_{x}-r<2^{M-1}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle(y_{1}\otimes x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({(n_{\eta}+(n_{x}-r)\times 2^{-M})\times 2^{-M+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}})}=\eta.

∎

Lemma 16

Let
K\in[(1+2^{-M+1})\times 2^{-M-2},1+2^{-2}-2^{-M}]_{\mathbb{F}}
.
Consider
\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-M-1
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
i\in[n]
, and
0<\alpha_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
, define
f:\mathbb{F}\rightarrow\mathbb{F}
as

f(x)\coloneq x\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.02228pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.55554pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\left({\alpha_{i}\otimes K}\right).

Then, there exists
n
and
\alpha_{i}
s such that one of the following statements holds:

f^{\sharp}\left({\langle-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},0\rangle}\right)\subset\langle-(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})^{-},(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})^{-}\rangle,\;f^{\sharp}\left(\langle\omega,1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\rangle\right)\subset\langle 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},(1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}+1})^{-}\rangle,

(40)

or

f^{\sharp}\left({\langle-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},0\rangle}\right)\subset\langle-(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})^{-},2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\rangle,\;f^{\sharp}\left(\langle\omega,1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\rangle\right)\subset\langle(2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})^{+},(1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}+1})^{-}\rangle.

(41)

If
\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}
, for each statement of
Equation

40
or
Equation

41
, there exist
n
and
\alpha_{i}
s such that satisfy each equation, respectively.

Additionally, if
2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\leq x\in\mathbb{F}
, then we have

f(x)\leq x\oplus\left({2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}}\right)^{+}.

(42)

Proof.

Since
f
is a sum of increasing functions, we only need to consider the endpoints of
\langle-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},0\rangle
and
\langle\omega,1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\rangle
, namely
x=0,-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}},1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}
, and
\omega
. In other words, we suffice to show

\displaystyle\begin{cases}f(-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})>-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(0)<2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(\omega)\geq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})<1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}+1}.\end{cases}

(43)

or

\displaystyle\begin{cases}f(-1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})>-2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(0)\leq 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(\omega)>2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}\\
f(1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}})<1.1\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}+1}.\end{cases}

(44)

Let
K
be represented as

K=\mathfrak{s}_{K}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{K}},\quad-M-2\leq\mathfrak{e}_{K}\leq 0.

By
Lemma

14
, at least one of
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\in(2^{-1},1]_{\mathbb{F}}
or
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\in[2^{-1},1)_{\mathbb{F}}
exists such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{K}

\displaystyle=1^{-}=1-2^{-M-1}=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\text{ times }},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{\parallel}\otimes\mathfrak{s}_{K}

\displaystyle=1.

We consider the following cases.

Case 1:
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}
exists.

In this case, we will show
Equation

43
.
First, define
m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}
as

\displaystyle m_{1}\coloneq\left\lceil{\frac{-1+\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}}{M+2}}\right\rceil_{\mathbb{Z}}\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}.

Then
m_{1}
is the unique non-negative natural number satisfying

\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)(m_{1}+1)\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M-1<\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1},

or equivalently,

\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+1,

(45)

where the first inequality is due to
\mathfrak{e}_{\min}-M-1<\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

For
i\in[m_{1}]
, we define
\beta_{i}
as

\beta_{i}\coloneq(2-2^{-M})\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)-1}=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\text{ times }}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)}.

As

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}_{\min}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\beta_{i}}=\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-1}\right)-1=\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}+M+1

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)-1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\max}-M-2,

we have
\beta_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
.
Define
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}
for
i\in[m_{1}]
as

\alpha^{\prime}_{i}\coloneq\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)-\mathfrak{e}_{K}}.

Since
\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}\in\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right)_{\mathbb{F}}
, we have
\mathfrak{s}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{i}}=2\mathfrak{s}_{K}^{{\dagger}}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{i}}=\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)-1
. Since
0\leq-\mathfrak{e}_{K}\leq M+2
, we have

\mathfrak{e}_{\min}\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\alpha^{\prime}_{i}}=\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-i}\right)-\mathfrak{e}_{K}-1\leq\mathfrak{e}_{\max}.

Therefore, we have
\alpha^{\prime}_{i}\in\mathbb{F}
, and

\alpha^{\prime}_{i}\otimes K=\beta_{i}.

(46)

Consider
2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}-\omega
.
By
Equation

45
,
2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}
is subnormal and

2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}-\omega=1.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}-1-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+M\text{ times }}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}-1}\in\mathbb{F}.

Since
2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}-\omega\in(-2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}},2^{1+\mathfrak{e}_{\min}})_{\mathbb{F}}
, by
Lemma

15
, there exist
\widetilde{\beta}_{1},\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}
such that

2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}-\omega=\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right).

(47)

We define
f_{1}(x)
as

f_{1}(x)\coloneq\begin{cases}x\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)\quad&\text{if}\quad m_{1}=0\\
x\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\alpha^{\prime}_{i}\otimes K\quad&\text{if}\quad m_{1}\geq 1\end{cases}

By
Equation

46
we have

f_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}x\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)&\text{if}\quad m_{1}=0\\
x\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{m_{1}}\beta_{i}&\text{if}\quad m_{1}\geq 1\end{cases}

Next, we present the following claim.

Claim 1-1:

For any
k\in[m_{1}]
, we have

\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)\oplus\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k}\beta_{i}=\beta_{k}.

We show the claim using the induction on
k
.

Base step (
k=1
):

By
Equation

47
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)\oplus\beta_{1}=\left({2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}-\omega}\right)\oplus 0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-1}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{rnd}{({0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}0\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}-1-\mathfrak{e}_{\min}+M\text{ times }}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-1}\right)}})}

\displaystyle=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-1}\right)}=\beta_{1}.

Induction step:

Assume that the induction hypothesis is satisfied for
k
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.00006pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\circ$}}\hss}{\sum}}{\vbox{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.5pt}{\scalebox{2.0}{$\scriptstyle\circ$}}\hss}}{\sum}}{\sum}{\sum}}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{k+1}\beta_{i}

\displaystyle=\beta_{k}\oplus\beta_{k+1}=\mathrm{rnd}{({0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{{M+1}\;\text{times}}0\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-k-1}\right)}})}

\displaystyle=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)\left({m_{1}-k-1}\right)}=\beta_{k+1}.

Therefore, we prove the claim for any
k\in[m_{1}]
.

Thus, we have

f_{1}(0)=\begin{cases}\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\otimes K\right)\oplus\left(\widetilde{\beta}_{2}\otimes K\right)=2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}-\omega<2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}&\text{if}\quad m_{1}=0,\\
\beta_{m_{1}}=0.\underbrace{1\dots 1}_{M+1\;\text{times}}\times 2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}<2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}}&\text{if}\quad m_{1}\geq 1.\end{cases}

(48)

Now, we present the following claim.

Claim 1-2:

We show the claim using the induction on

\displaystyle=\mathrm{rnd}{({\omega+\left({K\otimes\widetilde{\beta}_{1}\oplus K\otimes\widetilde{\beta}_{2}}\right)})}_{\mathbb{F}}=2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-\left({M+2}\right)m_{1}}.

\displaystyle=2^{\mathfrak{e}_{\zeta}-(M+2)(m_{1}-1)}.

Induction step:
